### 21. user (2026-07-04T08:42:26.976Z)

I found one more config-shape blocker in the staging gate.

The new local-upload staging path decides whether staging is possible with:

```python
input_root = storage_cfg.input_root
input_backend = storage_cfg.input_backend or "local"
if not input_root or input_backend == "local":
    return _failed(...)
```

That rejects a valid config where `storage.input_root` is itself an explicit cloud URI and `input_backend` is left at its default. The storage layer already treats an explicit root like `gs://in-bucket/videos` as backend-bearing (`resolve_input_uri` joins under `input_root` before consulting `input_backend`), and the model docs call out `input_root="gs://bucket/videos" + "x.mp4" => "gs://bucket/videos/x.mp4"`.

I reproduced on this head with:

```json
{
  "deployment": {"compute_target": "cloud_run"},
  "storage": {
    "backend": "gcs",
    "input_root": "gs://in-bucket/videos",
    "output_root": "gs://out-bucket/results"
  }
}
```

A local upload still fails pre-dispatch with "needs a cloud input ... or a cloud storage.input_root" because `input_backend` remains the default `local`, even though `input_root` is already a `gs://` destination.

Suggested fix: classify the configured `input_root` itself (e.g. `storage.parse_uri(input_root).backend in {"gcs", "s3", "gdrive-api"}` / at least not `local`) instead of relying on `input_backend`, and add a regression test where `input_root="gs://..."` is set without `input_backend="gcs"`.


=============== 469 ===============
One more issue: the new `storage.signed_url()` helper currently ignores the backend's configured `signed_url_ttl`.

Even though the active storage config is now threaded into `signed_reel_url()`, the helper signature defaults `ttl=3600` and always passes that value into the backend:

```python
def signed_url(..., ttl: int = 3600, ...):
    return get_storage(...).signed_url(ref, ttl=ttl, method=method)
```

Both `GCSStorage` and `S3Storage` only fall back to their configured `self._ttl` when `ttl` is falsey (`ttl or self._ttl`), so the configured TTL never gets a chance to apply for `/api/highlights`.

I reproduced locally on this head with an injected GCS client and `config={"gcs": {"signed_url_ttl": 123}}`; calling `storage.signed_url("gcs://bucket/highlights/vid/reel.mp4", config=..., method="GET")` passed an expiration of `3600` seconds, not `123`.

Suggested fix: make the helper's `ttl` optional/`None` and only pass an explicit ttl when the caller provides one, or pass `ttl=0/None` through so the backend uses its configured `signed_url_ttl`. Please add a small regression test that `/api/highlights` / `signed_reel_url()` honors a non-default `storage.gcs.signed_url_ttl` (and ideally S3 too, since it has the same pattern).


=============== 468 (blocker comment) ===============
I found one more blocker before I can re-LGTM.

`claim_due_job()` is CAS-safe for avoiding double-claims, but under parallel drain workers the losers return `None` (`backend/infrastructure/database_jobs.py:357-380`). `_drain_due_jobs()` then treats `None` as "queue empty" and exits (`backend/api/job_worker.py:273-276`). With `max_workers > 1`, multiple workers can select the same first queued row; one updates it, and the losers stop even though later jobs are still queued.

I reproduced locally on this head with 8 queued jobs and 8 concurrent claimers:

```text
[None, None, None, None, 'j0', None, None, 'j1'] -> only 2/8 claims
```

For long Cloud Run bucket-poll jobs this can leave dispatch capacity unused and queued jobs waiting until another submit/wake or a running worker finally frees up, which undermines the concurrency improvement this PR is trying to provide.

Suggested fix: make `claim_due_job()` retry on `rowcount != 1` until it either successfully claims a row or a fresh SELECT finds no runnable rows, or return a distinct lost-race sentinel so `_drain_due_jobs()` loops instead of treating it as empty. Please add a regression test with concurrent claimers/drains over N queued jobs proving there is no false-empty result / that N distinct jobs can be claimed.

    def seconds_until_next_due(self) -> Optional[float]:
        """Seconds until the soonest parked ('waiting') job becomes due, so the worker can
        schedule a wake-up (EXECUTION_POLICY.md P3 self-scheduling). Returns 0.0 if one is
        already due, or None if nothing is waiting. A NULL ``next_poll_at`` ⇒ due now (0.0)."""
        try:
            with self._connect() as conn:
                row = (
                    conn.cursor()
                    .execute(
                        "SELECT MIN(CASE WHEN next_poll_at IS NULL THEN 0 ELSE "
                        "  (julianday(next_poll_at) - julianday('now')) * 86400.0 END) AS secs "
                        "FROM jobs WHERE status='waiting'"
                    )
                    .fetchone()
                )
            if row is None or row["secs"] is None:
                return None
            return max(0.0, float(row["secs"]))
        except Exception as e:
            logger.error(f"Failed to compute next-due delay: {e}")
            return None

    def claim_due_job(self) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """Atomically claim ONE runnable job — 'queued', or 'waiting' whose ``next_poll_at`` is due —
        flipping it to 'running'. Compare-and-set on (id, status) so concurrent workers never
        double-claim (the DB row-claim is the only coordination needed; no master). Earliest-due
        first. Returns the claimed job dict, or None if nothing is runnable."""
        try:
            with self._connect() as conn:
                cur = conn.cursor()
                row = cur.execute(
                    "SELECT id, status FROM jobs WHERE status='queued' "
                    "OR (status='waiting' AND (next_poll_at IS NULL OR next_poll_at <= CURRENT_TIMESTAMP)) "
                    "ORDER BY COALESCE(next_poll_at, created_at) ASC LIMIT 1"
                ).fetchone()
                if not row:
                    return None
                cur.execute(
                    "UPDATE jobs SET status='running', started_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP "
                    "WHERE id=? AND status=?",
                    (row["id"], row["status"]),
                )
                conn.commit()
                if cur.rowcount != 1:
                    return None  # lost the race to a concurrent worker
            return self.get_job(row["id"])
        except Exception as e:
            logger.error(f"Failed to claim a due job: {e}")
            return None

    def fail_stale_jobs(self, reason: str = "interrupted by server restart") -> int:
        """Crash recovery: any job still queued/running from a previous process is dead
        (the executor is in-process). Mark failed so clients see a terminal state, not a
        hang. Returns the number of jobs swept.

        NOTE: ``recover_stale_jobs`` is the preferred startup call — it RE-QUEUES stale
        ``compile`` jobs (idempotent CPU stitch, safe to retry) instead of failing them. This
        blanket-fail method stays for callers/tests that want the old behaviour."""
        try:
            with self._connect() as conn:
                cur = conn.cursor()
                cur.execute(
                    "UPDATE jobs SET status='failed', error=?, finished_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP "
                    "WHERE status IN ('queued', 'running')",
                    (reason,),
                )

    row (carries ``owner_id``); ``result`` is the pipeline output."""

    flywheel_cfg = getattr(config, "flywheel", None)

    try:
        from backend import flywheel

        flywheel.run_for_job(job, result, config)

        logger.warning("Flywheel for job %s failed (non-fatal): %s", job.get("id"), e)


def _drain_due_jobs(config: Any, db: Any) -> int:
    """One worker tick: claim and run every currently-runnable job (queued, or waiting whose
    `next_poll_at` is due), earliest-due first, until the table has nothing runnable.

    Each parked job (`JobWaiting` → status 'waiting') drops out of this drain and is picked
    up by a FUTURE drain once due; we schedule that follow-up drain via `_schedule_drain` so
    a single-worker box keeps making progress with no central timer (the DB is the clock).
    Returns the number of jobs that reached a terminal/parked state this tick.
    """
    import backend.main as _main

    ran = 0
    while True:
        job = db.claim_due_job()
        if job is None:
            break
        ran += 1
        _main._run_claimed_job(job, config, db)
    # If anything is parked-but-not-yet-due, arrange a wake-up so it resumes without a new
    # submit. On the single-box executor this is a delayed re-drain; multi-worker just relies
    # on the next submit/drain. Best-effort — never raises into the worker.
    _main._schedule_next_drain_if_waiting(config, db)
    return ran


def _schedule_next_drain_if_waiting(config: Any, db: Any) -> None:
    """If any job is parked in 'waiting', arm a re-drain for when the soonest one is due, so a
    self-scheduled job resumes with no further client submit. The drain itself re-checks
    due-ness, so an early wake is harmless and a clamped wake for a far-future job is fine."""
    import backend.main as _main

    try:
        delay = db.seconds_until_next_due()
    except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover - defensive; never wedge the worker
        logger.error(f"Could not compute next-due delay: {e}")
        return
    if delay is None:
        return  # nothing waiting
    # Clamp so a far-future job doesn't pin a thread forever; the drain re-checks due-ness.
    _main._arm_wake_timer(max(0.0, min(delay, _main._MAX_DRAIN_WAKE_SEC)), config, db)


def _arm_wake_timer(delay_sec: float, config: Any, db: Any) -> None:
    """Arm a single daemon timer that re-submits a drain after ``delay_sec`` (no central
    scheduler — the DB is the clock). Isolated as the sole timer-creating seam so tests can
    monkeypatch it to a no-op and assert the *scheduling decision* without real waiting."""

        delay_sec,

def _backend_class(name: str):
    """Lazy-resolve a backend class so cloud SDKs aren't imported until needed."""
    if name == "local":
        from backend.storage.local import LocalStorage

        return LocalStorage
    if name == "s3":
        from backend.storage.s3 import S3Storage

        return S3Storage
    if name == "gcs":
        from backend.storage.gcs import GCSStorage

        return GCSStorage
    if name == "gdrive":
        from backend.storage.gdrive import GDriveStorage

        return GDriveStorage
    if name == "gdrive-api":
        from backend.storage.gdrive_api import GDriveApiStorage

        return GDriveApiStorage
    raise ValueError(
        f"unknown storage backend: {name!r} (expected local|s3|gcs|gdrive|gdrive-api)"
    )


def get_storage(
    backend: Optional[str] = None,
    *,
    config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
    **overrides,
) -> StorageBackend:
    """Construct a backend. ``backend`` (or ``config['backend']``, default 'local') selects
    the implementation; its per-backend settings come from ``config[backend]`` merged with
    ``overrides``. Settings carry NO secrets — only endpoints/regions/paths/source-selectors."""
    cfg = config or {}
    name = backend or cfg.get("backend", "local")
    # Per-backend settings live under the backend name; tolerate the hyphen→underscore form so a
    # hyphenated backend ("gdrive-api") can read a pydantic field that can't contain a hyphen
    # ("gdrive_api"). Hyphen-free names are unaffected (the first lookup wins).
    settings: Dict[str, Any] = dict(
        cfg.get(name) or cfg.get(name.replace("-", "_")) or {}
    )
    settings.update(overrides)
    return _backend_class(name)(**settings)


def fetch(
    uri: str,
    dst: "str | os.PathLike[str] | None" = None,
    *,
    config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
    overwrite: bool = False,
) -> str:
    """Resolve ``uri`` to a local file path. For ``local`` this is an identity passthrough
    (no copy); for s3/gcs it downloads to ``dst``."""
    ref = parse_uri(uri)
    return get_storage(ref.backend, config=config).fetch_to_local(
        ref, dst, overwrite=overwrite
    )


def put(
    local_path: "str | os.PathLike[str]",
    uri: str,
    *,
    config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
    content_type: Optional[str] = None,
) -> str:
    """Upload/copy ``local_path`` to ``uri``; return ``uri``."""
    ref = parse_uri(uri)
    get_storage(ref.backend, config=config).put(
        local_path, ref, content_type=content_type
    )
    return uri


def exists(uri: str, *, config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None) -> bool:
    """True if the object at ``uri`` exists (a bucket object or a local file)."""
    ref = parse_uri(uri)
    return bool(get_storage(ref.backend, config=config).exists(ref))


def signed_url(
    uri: str,
    *,
    config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
    ttl: int = 3600,
    method: str = "GET",
) -> str:
    """A time-limited signed URL for ``uri`` — the client fetches bytes DIRECTLY from the store, off
    the app's response path (so a large object isn't streamed back through the server; #463). Backends
    without object signing (e.g. local) raise ``NotImplementedError``."""
    ref = parse_uri(uri)
    return get_storage(ref.backend, config=config).signed_url(ref, ttl=ttl, method=method)


def list_uris(
    prefix_uri: str, *, config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None

def input_is_local(raw: str, storage_config: Any = None) -> bool:
    """True if ``raw`` resolves (under the input config) to a LOCAL filesystem path — i.e. a
    plain on-disk file the caller can stat/validate directly. False for a bucket / Drive URI
    that must be fetched first. Raises ``ValueError`` for an unsupported scheme (same as
    ``resolve_input_ref``)."""
    return resolve_input_ref(raw, storage_config).backend == "local"


def acquire_local_input(
    raw: str, storage_config: Any = None, *, scratch_parent: "str | None" = None
) -> str:
    """Resolve ``raw`` (a path or storage URI) to a LOCAL file path ready for processing.

    Local / bare paths are returned UNCHANGED (identity passthrough, no copy — today's behaviour
    byte-for-byte). A bucket / Drive source (``gs://``/``s3://``/``gdrive://``, or a bare name under a
    non-local ``input_root``/``input_backend``) is downloaded into a fresh scratch dir and the local
    path returned. The caller owns successful remote scratch copies; use
    ``cleanup_acquired_local_input`` when the consumer has finished reading the returned path."""
    sc = _storage_dict(storage_config)
    ref = resolve_input_ref(raw, sc)
    if ref.backend == "local":
        return (
            ref.key
        )  # identity — no temp, no copy, byte-identical to passing the path directly
    scratch = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="rally-input-", dir=scratch_parent)
    try:
        return fetch(ref.uri, os.path.join(scratch, ref.name or "in.mp4"), config=sc)
    except Exception:
        shutil.rmtree(scratch, ignore_errors=True)
        raise


def cleanup_acquired_local_input(
    raw: str, local_path: str, storage_config: Any = None
) -> None:
    """Delete a remote scratch dir produced by ``acquire_local_input``.

    Local inputs are identity paths and must never be removed. Remote acquisitions are downloaded
    into a fresh ``rally-input-*`` directory; deleting that directory is safe once the caller has
    finished hashing/validating/stitching/segmenting the returned file.
    """
    try:
        if input_is_local(raw, storage_config):
            return
    except ValueError:
        return
    if not local_path:
        return
    scratch = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(local_path))
    if os.path.basename(scratch).startswith("rally-input-") and os.path.isdir(scratch):
        shutil.rmtree(scratch, ignore_errors=True)

### 40. user (2026-07-04T08:48:44.210Z)

# Cloud Run GPU **Job** image for the rally worker (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M6/M7, ADR-012/D16).
#
# Runs `python -m backend.worker infer …` INLINE on an L4 (detect + window + stitch co-located so
# CPU/wall + egress are metered into one job — D4). Built + pushed + verified by the owner on the
# M7 real run (the $100/₹10k budget, D17); CI does NOT build this (a CUDA image is too heavy) — the
# dispatch shim is proven with a fake client (tests/test_compute_backend.py).
#
# Two python stacks, deliberately separate (a framework conflict otherwise — same split as the
# native GPU box, deploy/digitalocean/gpu_setup.sh):
#   * the MAIN app env (this repo + ffmpeg) drives the pipeline + stitching;
#   * a conda `wasb` env (py3.8 + torch 1.11.0+cu113) runs the WASB shuttle detector as a subprocess
#     (the native runner shells out to $WASB_PYTHON), so the app's torch never co-installs with it.
#
# ⚠ WASB-C3: the pretrained badminton weights are academic-data-trained — provenance NOT cleared for
#   commercial sharing. They are NOT baked into this image; stage them at runtime (a bucket fetch or a
#   mount) and point $WASB_WEIGHTS at them. Resolve WASB-C3 before any public, non-owner serving.

FROM nvidia/cuda:12.4.1-runtime-ubuntu22.04

ENV DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive \
    PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
    PIP_NO_CACHE_DIR=1 \
    CONDA_DIR=/opt/conda \
    WASB_REPO_DIR=/opt/models/WASB-SBDT

# ffmpeg (stitch/decode) + DejaVu fonts (the watermark filter) + git/curl for the WASB env build.
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
        ffmpeg fonts-dejavu-core git curl ca-certificates build-essential \
        python3 python3-pip python3-venv \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# --- WASB detector env (micromamba + conda-forge — BSD-licensed, NO Anaconda ToS; P3a, ADR-005) ---
# Enforces ADR-005 (every dependency — code, data, weights, AND the build toolchain — stays
# MIT/Apache/BSD): micromamba (BSD-3) + conda-forge (community) replaces Miniconda's Anaconda-ToS-gated
# `defaults` channels (the ≥200-employee Terms-of-Service trap flagged in PACKAGING_PLAN.md §3).
# MAMBA_ROOT_PREFIX=$CONDA_DIR (=/opt/conda) keeps the env at $CONDA_DIR/envs/wasb so $WASB_PYTHON below
# is UNCHANGED. Same verified torch-1.11.0+cu113 WASB stack; the pip wheels are unaffected by the switch.
ENV MAMBA_ROOT_PREFIX=/opt/conda
RUN curl -sSL https://micro.mamba.pm/api/micromamba/linux-64/latest \
        | tar -xj -C /usr/local bin/micromamba \
    && micromamba create -y -q -n wasb -c conda-forge python=3.8 \
    && git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/nttcom/WASB-SBDT.git "$WASB_REPO_DIR" \
    && "$CONDA_DIR/envs/wasb/bin/python" -m pip install -q \
        torch==1.11.0+cu113 torchvision==0.12.0+cu113 --extra-index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu113 \
    && ( [ -f "$WASB_REPO_DIR/requirements.txt" ] && "$CONDA_DIR/envs/wasb/bin/python" -m pip install -q -r "$WASB_REPO_DIR/requirements.txt" || true ) \
    && "$CONDA_DIR/envs/wasb/bin/python" -m pip install -q \
        hydra-core omegaconf pandas opencv-python-headless pillow scipy tqdm pyyaml matplotlib "numpy<1.24"

# --- Main application env ---
WORKDIR /app
COPY pyproject.toml README.md ./
COPY requirements-trackers.txt ./
COPY backend ./backend
COPY training ./training
# Install with the [storage-gcs,cloud-run] extras. storage-gcs: the cloud worker PULLS the gs://
# input and PUSHES the gs:// outputs/done-marker through backend.storage (google-cloud-storage is
# lazy-imported there); without it the job dies at the first gs:// fetch with `ModuleNotFoundError:
# google.cloud`. cloud-run: this SAME image is reused as the deployed CONTROL PLANE, which dispatches
# the L4 worker Job via google.cloud.run_v2 (backend/compute/cloud_run.py, lazy-imported); without it
# the control plane fails every dispatch with "google-cloud-run is required for CloudRunCompute's real
# client". Both are real Cloud Run failures the mock-client tests can't see (the SDKs are faked in CI).
# D13/P12: `trackers` (ByteTrack's new home) is a git-only dep (broken PyPI wheel —
# roboflow/trackers#474) so it's NOT pulled by `pip install .`; install it out-of-band before
# the main env, or the person-cue path crashes with ModuleNotFoundError. The immutable git SHA is
# single-sourced in requirements-trackers.txt.
RUN python3 -m pip install --upgrade pip \
    && python3 -m pip install -r requirements-trackers.txt \
    && python3 -m pip install ".[storage-gcs,cloud-run]"

# The native runner finds the WASB env + repo by env (no `conda activate` needed); weights come in at
# runtime (WASB-C3). detector_impl=native = the Linux-native WASB runner; cuda stays the default.
# PYTHONUNBUFFERED: without it the worker's stdout/stderr sit in the block buffer until process
# exit, so Cloud Logging shows a single end-of-run burst instead of live progress (observed on the
# first real M7 runs, 2026-07-03) — a running job looks stalled and mid-run failures log nothing.
ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
    WASB_PYTHON=/opt/conda/envs/wasb/bin/python \
    WASB_REPO_DIR=/opt/models/WASB-SBDT \
    WASB_WEIGHTS=/opt/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar \
    RALLY_DETECTOR_IMPL=native

# The dispatcher (CloudRunCompute) supplies the per-execution args:
#   infer --video-uri gs://… --out-uri gs://… --done-uri gs://… --segmenter wasb_hybrid --set …
ENTRYPOINT ["python3", "-m", "backend.worker"]
=== extras in pyproject ===
# Test + lint/type tooling — matches the CI lanes (.github/workflows/ci.yml).
dev = [
    "pytest",
    "pytest-cov",
    "httpx",        # fastapi TestClient transport
    "ruff",
    "mypy",
    "PyJWT[crypto]>=2.8",  # M9 edge-auth: Cf-Access JWT verify (RS256) — tests + CI
]

# M9 edge-auth (Cloudflare Access). DEFAULT-OFF: only needed when security.edge_auth_enabled=True.
# `jwt` is lazy-imported, so the base runtime works without this; install it for hosted/multi-tenant.
auth = ["PyJWT[crypto]>=2.8"]

# Per-video observability reports (SOFT dependency: the pipeline degrades to a
# no-op if absent). Pinned to the canonical private repo's immutable commit
# SHA; fetched over HTTPS. If you lack access, simply omit this group — reporting
# tests skip via pytest.importorskip. For local dev against a sibling checkout:
#   pip install -e ../sports-obsreport --no-build-isolation
reporting = [
    "obsreport @ git+https://github.com/Khelsutra/sports-obsreport.git@a0c7252c6e4159f8702360bea1cb46e5148b5c9f",
]

# `gpu` is DOCUMENTATION ONLY — it is intentionally empty. torch/torchvision
# CUDA wheels require `--index-url`/`--extra-index-url`, which PEP 621 cannot
# express, so they are NOT declarable here. Install them out-of-band instead:
#   # CUDA 12.4 box:
#   pip install torch>=2.12.0 torchvision>=0.27.0 --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu124
#   # CPU-only box (also what CI uses):
#   pip install torch torchvision --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu
gpu = []

# Storage backends (docs/DECOUPLED_COMPUTE/). The default `local` backend needs NONE of
# these. Cloud SDKs are imported lazily, so `import backend.storage` works without them;
# install the extra for the bucket you use. (boto3's S3 client covers AWS + Cloudflare R2 +
# DO Spaces + MinIO via endpoint_url.) `gdrive://` needs NO extra — it reads a locally
# MOUNTED Google Drive (Google Drive for Desktop) as plain files; see backend/storage/gdrive.py.
storage-s3 = ["boto3>=1.34"]
storage-gcs = ["google-cloud-storage>=2.16"]

### 45. user (2026-07-04T08:49:56.368Z)

`compute_target=cloud_run`). `get_compute_backend(config)` then builds `CloudRunCompute` from env:
```bash
export DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving
export CLOUD_RUN_JOB=rally-worker  CLOUD_RUN_REGION=$REGION  GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=$PROJECT
# A `worker infer` (or the API job path) with a gs:// video now DISPATCHES to the Job instead of
# running in-process; the container runs the SAME worker INLINE (compute_target forced local_gpu).
python -m backend.worker infer --video-uri gs://<bucket>/videos/clip.mp4 --out-uri gs://<bucket> --segmenter wasb_hybrid
```
**The AI handoff needs its key IN THE JOB env** (`GEMINI_API_KEY` — mount a Secret Manager secret
on the job), or run fully-local with `--set indexing.skip_ai_handoff=true` (candidate windows
emitted as rallies — the eval-parity mode). With neither, the segmenter used to bail to a silent
0-rally "success" **after** the paid GPU pass; the cloud-serving startup checks now fail this at
boot (`AI_HANDOFF_KEY_MISSING`, rc 2) before the video is even pulled.

### 3a. Control-plane SERVICE deploy — required flags for reliable async jobs

The API server (`/api/process`, `/api/compile` — #329) runs its **job worker IN-PROCESS**: the
request returns `202` and a background thread drains the job (GPU detection dispatches to the Job;
the reel stitch runs on the control plane itself). On Cloud Run that background thread only makes
progress while the instance has CPU, so a scale-to-zero **service** deploy MUST set:
```bash
gcloud run deploy rally-control-plane --image <image> --region $REGION \
  --no-cpu-throttling \        # CPU stays allocated between requests → the drain thread finishes
  --min-instances 1 \          # one always-warm instance drains reliably (cheap CPU; NOT the D7 GPU pool)
  --cpu 2 --memory 4Gi --port 8080 --allow-unauthenticated \
  --set-env-vars DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving,CLOUD_RUN_JOB=rally-worker,CLOUD_RUN_REGION=$REGION,GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=$PROJECT
```
Without `--no-cpu-throttling`, a `/api/compile` returns 202 and then **stalls** — the reel never
finishes and startup crash-recovery would mark it terminal. `min-instances 1` does NOT contradict
D7 (that was the ~$500/mo warm **GPU** pool); this is a ~1-vCPU CPU instance. Defense-in-depth is in
the code: `Database.recover_stale_jobs()` **re-queues** a compile interrupted by a restart (idempotent
stitch) rather than failing it, so even an unlucky scale-down mid-stitch self-heals on the next drain.
Reserve `--allow-unauthenticated` for the pre-CF-auth smoke test only; lock ingress to the CF proxy
(M9) before inviting testers.

## 4. M7 acceptance (the owner runlog)
Capture a `DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_<date>.md` under
`docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/` (mirrors the truly-local template): the exact commands,
rally counts, `gpu_active_seconds`/`wall_seconds`/`bytes_out`, a reel smoke-test (`ffmpeg -f null -`
exit 0 + a 5-frame contact sheet), gotchas, and the **$0 teardown audit**.

## Gotchas
- The dispatched container **must run inline** — `CloudRunCompute` forces `compute_target=local_gpu`
  + `indexing.detector_impl=native` in the per-execution args so the Job never re-dispatches itself.
- Completion is a **bucket marker** (`<out_uri>/_dispatch/<token>.done`), not the Cloud Run API status

         """Atomically claim ONE runnable job — 'queued', or 'waiting' whose ``next_poll_at`` is due —
         flipping it to 'running'. Compare-and-set on (id, status) so concurrent workers never

+        first. Returns the claimed job dict, or None ONLY when a fresh SELECT finds nothing

+        A lost CAS RETRIES instead of returning: under the parallel drain (#466) several workers
+        can SELECT the same head-of-queue row, and if the losers reported None the caller
+        (``_drain_due_jobs``) would read it as "queue empty" and stop with runnable jobs still
+        queued — stranding dispatch capacity until some later submit/wake. The retry loop is
+        deliberately UNBOUNDED: every lost race means another worker successfully claimed that
+        row, so the runnable set shrank and global progress is guaranteed (lock-free progress);
+        a retry cap would just reintroduce the false-empty. One connection serves all attempts —
+        the SELECT is an autocommit read and each CAS commits before the next iteration, so no

+                while True:

+                        "ORDER BY COALESCE(next_poll_at, created_at) ASC LIMIT 1"
+                    ).fetchone()
+                    if not row:
+                        return None  # nothing runnable — a TRUE empty, not a lost race
+                    cur.execute(

diff --git a/tests/test_job_worker_loop.py b/tests/test_job_worker_loop.py

@@ -13,13 +13,16 @@ Covered:
 - a `JobWaiting` parks the job (no terminal state) and a later drain (once due) resumes it;

+  timer seam stubbed — no real waiting, no leaked threads;

+  false-empty: a lost CAS race retries inside `claim_due_job`, so None means truly empty.
 
 The real-timer seam (`_arm_wake_timer`) is monkeypatched to a recorder in every test that
 can park a job, so nothing actually sleeps and no daemon timer outlives the test.

 import sqlite3
+import threading
 
 import pytest
 
@@ -222,6 +225,44 @@ def test_claim_orders_earliest_due_first(env):
     assert set(order) == {"a", "b"}
 
 
+# --- concurrent claimers: no false-empty, no double-claim (#466/#468 parallel drain) ------
+
+
+def test_concurrent_claimers_claim_all_jobs_distinctly(tmp_path):
+    """Regression for the #468 review blocker: under the parallel drain, N claimers racing
+    over N queued jobs can SELECT the same head-of-queue row; only one CAS wins. Before the
+    lost-race retry, each loser returned None — which `_drain_due_jobs` reads as "queue
+    empty" — so a single tick stranded runnable jobs (observed 2/8 claimed). Now every
+    claimer must walk away with a job while any is runnable: all N results are non-None and
+    the N claimed ids are distinct."""
+    db = Database(str(tmp_path / "race.db"))
+    n = 8
+    for i in range(n):
+        db.create_job(f"j{i}", f"/v{i}.mp4")
+
+    barrier = threading.Barrier(n)
+    results: list = [None] * n
+
+    def _claim(slot: int) -> None:
+        barrier.wait()  # release all claimers together so they genuinely race on the head row
+        results[slot] = db.claim_due_job()
+
+    threads = [threading.Thread(target=_claim, args=(i,)) for i in range(n)]
+    for t in threads:
+        t.start()
+    for t in threads:
+        t.join()
+
+    claimed_ids = [r["id"] if r else None for r in results]
+    # No false-empty: with N runnable jobs, no claimer may come back empty-handed.
+    assert all(r is not None for r in results), f"lost-race leaked None: {claimed_ids}"
+    # No double-claim: the N winners are exactly the N distinct queued jobs.
+    assert sorted(claimed_ids) == [f"j{i}" for i in range(n)]
+    for i in range(n):
+        assert db.get_job(f"j{i}")["status"] == "running"
+    assert db.claim_due_job() is None  # and None still means TRULY empty afterwards
+
+
 # --- seconds_until_next_due (the wake-delay computation) ----------------------------------
 
 
 M backend/infrastructure/database_jobs.py
 M tests/test_job_worker_loop.py

+    cloud_env, tmp_path, monkeypatch
+):
+    """input_root='gs://…' with input_backend left at its DEFAULT ('local') is a valid cloud config —
+    the storage layer classifies an explicit root by its scheme (resolve_input_uri joins under
+    input_root before ever consulting input_backend), so the staging gate must too (PR #467 review).
+    Regression: the gate previously required input_backend != 'local' and hard-failed this shape."""

+
+    def _fake_put(src, uri, **kw):
+        puts.append((str(src), uri))
+        return uri
+

+    local_path = str(tmp_path / "upload.mp4")
+
+    out = m._execute_processing(

+    src, dest = puts[0]
+    assert src == local_path
+    assert dest.startswith("gs://in-bucket/videos/") and dest.endswith("/upload.mp4")
+    # dispatch continued from the STAGED gs:// URI

+    argv = fake.calls[0]
+    assert argv[argv.index("--video-uri") + 1] == dest

 def test_cloud_local_upload_without_input_root_still_fails(cloud_env, tmp_path, monkeypatch):

         return self._client.generate_presigned_url(
             op,
diff --git a/tests/test_storage.py b/tests/test_storage.py
index 8d7b427..11f1875 100644
--- a/tests/test_storage.py
+++ b/tests/test_storage.py
@@ -250,7 +250,10 @@ class _FakeBlob:
         self.store.pop((self.bucket, self.name), None)
 
     def generate_signed_url(self, version, expiration, method):
-        return f"https://gcs.example/{self.bucket}/{self.name}?version={version}"
+        # Expose the expiration so TTL-plumbing tests can assert the configured
+        # signed_url_ttl actually reaches the signer (#469).
+        ttl = int(expiration.total_seconds())
+        return f"https://gcs.example/{self.bucket}/{self.name}?version={version}&ttl={ttl}"
 
 
 class _FakeGCSClient:
@@ -309,6 +312,39 @@ def test_gcs_backend_roundtrip_with_fake_client(tmp_path):
     assert not be.exists(ref)
 
 
+# --------------------------------------------------- module signed_url helper: TTL plumbing (#469)
+# The helper's ttl default must be None so the backend's configured signed_url_ttl applies —
+# a hardcoded helper default would silently shadow the operator's config for every caller
+# that doesn't pass an explicit ttl (e.g. /api/highlights via signed_reel_url).
+
+
+def test_module_signed_url_honors_configured_gcs_ttl():
+    # Reviewer repro on #469: config={'gcs': {'signed_url_ttl': 123}} must reach the signer.
+    url = storage.signed_url(
+        "gcs://bucket/highlights/vid/reel.mp4",
+        config={"gcs": {"client": _FakeGCSClient(), "signed_url_ttl": 123}},
+        method="GET",
+    )
+    assert "ttl=123" in url
+
+
+def test_module_signed_url_honors_configured_s3_ttl():
+    url = storage.signed_url(
+        "s3://bucket/highlights/vid/reel.mp4",
+        config={"s3": {"client": _FakeS3Client(), "signed_url_ttl": 456}},
+    )
+    assert "X-Amz-Expires=456" in url
+
+
+def test_module_signed_url_explicit_ttl_overrides_config():
+    url = storage.signed_url(
+        "gcs://bucket/x",
+        config={"gcs": {"client": _FakeGCSClient(), "signed_url_ttl": 123}},
+        ttl=999,
+    )
+    assert "ttl=999" in url
+
+
 # --------------------------------------------------------------- Google Drive (local mount)
 # The backend reads a locally MOUNTED Google Drive (Google Drive for Desktop). A tmp_path
 # folder stands in for the mount's "My Drive" root — no network, no Drive API, no extra deps.
diff --git a/tests/test_upload_download.py b/tests/test_upload_download.py
index b258a13..14b78d6 100644
--- a/tests/test_upload_download.py
+++ b/tests/test_upload_download.py
@@ -329,6 +329,35 @@ def test_highlights_redirects_to_signed_url_when_reel_in_bucket(env, monkeypatch
     assert seen.get("sign_cfg") is not None and seen["sign_cfg"].get("output_root") == "gs://b/out"
 
 
+def test_highlights_signed_url_honors_configured_gcs_ttl(env):
+    """/api/highlights must sign with the operator's storage.gcs.signed_url_ttl, not a hardcoded
+    3600 (#469 review). Exercises the REAL storage layer end-to-end (config → get_storage →
+    GCSStorage.signed_url) with an injected fake client — only the GCS SDK is faked."""
+
+    class _Blob:
+        def __init__(self, bucket, key):
+            self.bucket, self.key = bucket, key
+
+        def exists(self):
+            return self.key == "out/highlights/vid469/reel.mp4"
+
+        def generate_signed_url(self, version, expiration, method):
+            ttl = int(expiration.total_seconds())
+            return f"https://gcs.example/{self.bucket}/{self.key}?ttl={ttl}"
+
+    class _Client:
+        def bucket(self, name):
+            return type("_B", (), {"blob": lambda _s, key: _Blob(name, key)})()
+
+    client, db, _tmp, cfg = env
+    cfg.storage.output_root = "gs://b/out"
+    cfg.storage.gcs = {"client": _Client(), "signed_url_ttl": 123}
+    db.add_video("vid469", "gs://b/in/x.mp4", "processed", [])
+    r = client.get("/api/highlights/vid469/reel.mp4", follow_redirects=False)
+    assert r.status_code == 307
+    assert r.headers["location"].endswith("?ttl=123")
+
+
 def test_highlights_falls_back_to_local_when_no_bucket_reel(env, monkeypatch):
     """No mirrored bucket object (or a signing hiccup) → serve the local file, unchanged behaviour."""
     client, db, _tmp, cfg = env

### 102. assistant (2026-07-04T09:16:48.269Z)

"""Generate the control-plane Cloud Run *service manifest* (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M8/M9, #473).

The A1 alpha deploy drove `rally-control-plane` from a hand-generated YAML that lived in a
session scratchpad — this commits the generator so the deploy is reproducible from a clean
clone. It emits a Knative service manifest for::

    python deploy/cloudrun/gen_service_yaml.py --edge-auth off -o service.yaml
    gcloud run services replace service.yaml --region asia-southeast1

Why a MANIFEST (`services replace`) and not `gcloud run deploy` flags: the container command is
a /bin/bash wrapper carrying shell metacharacters (pipes, redirects, &&) — on Windows the gcloud
cmd-shim mangles those through `--command/--args`, while a YAML round-trips them verbatim. Run
gcloud from PowerShell, not git-bash (MSYS rewrites `/tmp`-style paths).

The app is configured through a config.json baked INTO the manifest: the wrapper base64-decodes
it to `/tmp/apphome/config.json` and exports `RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/apphome` before exec'ing the
server, so one image serves every config shape (config.json wins over the cloud-serving preset —
backend/config/loader.py). `--edge-auth` is REQUIRED and explicit: `off` is only for the
identity-token smoke/e2e window; redeploy with `on` (the Cf-Access JWT verify, M9) before the
service faces testers again.

Deliberate manifest choices (learned on the A1 deploy):
  * `run.googleapis.com/cpu-throttling: "false"` + minScale 1 — the in-process job worker drains
    on a background thread; without always-allocated CPU a 202'd compile stalls (README §3a).
  * maxScale DEFAULTS TO 1 — the jobs/videos DB is per-instance ephemeral SQLite, so a second
    instance splits the queue and `/api/jobs/{id}` polls (issue #471, option 1). Raise it only
    once state is shared.
  * `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is NOT set — it's a reserved runtime env name and the manifest is rejected
    with it; the code default ("rally-worker") is already correct.
  * No secrets here: bucket/project/aud/team-domain are the same non-secret identifiers the
    committed runlogs carry; the Gemini key stays in Secret Manager (the alpha runs
    `skip_ai_handoff=true` and never needs it).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import base64
import json
import sys

DEFAULT_PROJECT = "gen-lang-client-0306026826"
DEFAULT_REGION = "asia-southeast1"
DEFAULT_SERVICE = "rally-control-plane"
DEFAULT_SERVING_BUCKET = "gs://khelsutra-rally-serving"
DEFAULT_CF_TEAM_DOMAIN = "https://khelsutra.cloudflareaccess.com"
DEFAULT_CF_ACCESS_AUD = "c980da67ed888fff935fcd913ae1eb4da63f9d65805d6e1c5ca76f92f517082b"


def build_config(args: argparse.Namespace) -> dict:
    """The app config the wrapper materializes at RALLY_APP_HOME/config.json."""
    bucket = args.serving_bucket.rstrip("/")
    return {
        "deployment": {"profile": "cloud-serving", "compute_target": "cloud_run"},
        # WASB-only alpha: candidate windows ARE the rallies; no Gemini key in this service.
        "indexing": {"skip_ai_handoff": True},
        "storage": {
            "backend": "gcs",
            "output_root": bucket,
            "input_backend": "gcs",
            "input_root": f"{bucket}/videos",
        },
        "security": {
            "edge_auth_enabled": args.edge_auth == "on",
            "cf_team_domain": args.cf_team_domain,
            "cf_access_aud": args.cf_access_aud,
            "allowed_video_root": "/tmp/apphome/output",
        },
    }


def build_yaml(args: argparse.Namespace) -> str:
    """Render the Knative service manifest for `gcloud run services replace`."""
    cfg_b64 = base64.b64encode(
        json.dumps(build_config(args), separators=(",", ":")).encode("utf-8")
    ).decode("ascii")
    # mkdir BEFORE the redirect (the redirect can't create the directory), then exec so the
    # server is PID 1 and Cloud Run's SIGTERM reaches it directly.
    wrapper = (
        "mkdir -p /tmp/apphome && "
        f"printf %s '{cfg_b64}' | base64 -d > /tmp/apphome/config.json && "
        "export RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/apphome && "
        "exec python3 -m backend.main --server --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8080"
    )
    return f"""\
apiVersion: serving.knative.dev/v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: {args.service}
  labels:
    cloud.googleapis.com/location: {args.region}
spec:
  template:
    metadata:
      annotations:
        autoscaling.knative.dev/minScale: "{args.min_instances}"
        autoscaling.knative.dev/maxScale: "{args.max_instances}"
        run.googleapis.com/cpu-throttling: "false"
    spec:
      timeoutSeconds: {args.timeout}
      containers:
        - image: {args.image}
          command: ["/bin/bash"]
          args: ["-c", "{wrapper}"]
          ports:
            - containerPort: 8080
          resources:
            limits:
              cpu: "{args.cpu}"
              memory: {args.memory}
          env:
            - name: DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE
              value: cloud-serving
            - name: CLOUD_RUN_REGION
              value: {args.region}
            - name: GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT
              value: {args.project}
            - name: RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS
              value: "*"
"""


def main(argv: "list[str] | None" = None) -> int:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    p.add_argument(
        "--edge-auth",
        choices=("on", "off"),
        required=True,
        help="REQUIRED and explicit: 'off' only for the identity-token smoke/e2e window; "
        "redeploy 'on' (M9 Cf-Access verify) before testers touch it again.",
    )
    p.add_argument("--project", default=DEFAULT_PROJECT)
    p.add_argument("--region", default=DEFAULT_REGION)
    p.add_argument("--service", default=DEFAULT_SERVICE)
    p.add_argument(
        "--image",
        default=None,
        help="Container image; default = the project's rally-worker:latest (post-#470 it "
        "carries cloud-run + auth, so the worker image IS the control-plane image).",
    )
    p.add_argument("--serving-bucket", default=DEFAULT_SERVING_BUCKET)
    p.add_argument("--cf-team-domain", default=DEFAULT_CF_TEAM_DOMAIN)
    p.add_argument("--cf-access-aud", default=DEFAULT_CF_ACCESS_AUD)
    p.add_argument("--min-instances", type=int, default=1)
    p.add_argument(
        "--max-instances",
        type=int,
        default=1,
        help="Default 1: per-instance SQLite state splits across instances (issue #471).",
    )
    p.add_argument("--cpu", type=int, default=2)
    p.add_argument("--memory", default="4Gi")
    p.add_argument("--timeout", type=int, default=300)
    p.add_argument("-o", "--output", default="-", help="Output path, or '-' for stdout.")
    args = p.parse_args(argv)
    if args.image is None:
        args.image = f"{args.region}-docker.pkg.dev/{args.project}/rally/rally-worker:latest"

    text = build_yaml(args)
    if args.output == "-":
        sys.stdout.write(text)
    else:
        with open(args.output, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
            f.write(text)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

### 105. assistant (2026-07-04T09:17:17.133Z)

"""Structural guard for the control-plane service-manifest generator (#473).

CI can't `gcloud run services replace` anything, so — like the Dockerfile guard in
test_compute_backend.py — lock the manifest's load-bearing shape structurally: the baked
config.json must round-trip through the base64 wrapper byte-exactly (that's the piece a
hand-edit silently corrupts), the always-allocated-CPU/scale annotations must survive
(README §3a: without them a 202'd compile stalls), and the reserved `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` env
name must never re-appear (a manifest carrying it is rejected at deploy time).

The generator is exercised through its real CLI (a subprocess), not an import — deploy/
is not a package, and the CLI is exactly what a deployer runs.
"""

import base64
import json
import re
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

_GEN = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "deploy" / "cloudrun" / "gen_service_yaml.py"


def _generate(*extra: str) -> str:
    out = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, str(_GEN), *extra],
        capture_output=True,
        text=True,
        check=True,
    )
    return out.stdout


def _baked_config(manifest: str) -> dict:
    """Decode the config.json the wrapper bakes into the manifest."""
    m = re.search(r"printf %s '([A-Za-z0-9+/=]+)' \| base64 -d", manifest)
    assert m, "wrapper must carry a base64 config payload"
    return json.loads(base64.b64decode(m.group(1)))


def test_manifest_bakes_the_cloud_serving_config():
    cfg = _baked_config(_generate("--edge-auth", "off"))
    assert cfg["deployment"] == {"profile": "cloud-serving", "compute_target": "cloud_run"}
    # WASB-only alpha: no Gemini key in this service, so the handoff must be off.
    assert cfg["indexing"]["skip_ai_handoff"] is True
    assert cfg["storage"]["backend"] == "gcs"
    assert cfg["storage"]["output_root"] == "gs://khelsutra-rally-serving"
    assert cfg["storage"]["input_root"] == "gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos"
    assert cfg["security"]["edge_auth_enabled"] is False
    assert cfg["security"]["allowed_video_root"] == "/tmp/apphome/output"


def test_manifest_edge_auth_toggle_is_explicit():
    on = _baked_config(_generate("--edge-auth", "on"))
    assert on["security"]["edge_auth_enabled"] is True
    assert on["security"]["cf_team_domain"].startswith("https://")
    assert len(on["security"]["cf_access_aud"]) == 64  # the Access-app aud is a sha256 hex
    # --edge-auth is required: generating without choosing must fail, not default silently.
    rc = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, str(_GEN)], capture_output=True, text=True
    ).returncode
    assert rc != 0


def test_manifest_has_the_reliability_flags_and_no_reserved_env():
    text = _generate("--edge-auth", "off")
    # README §3a: the in-process drain needs always-allocated CPU + a warm instance.
    assert 'run.googleapis.com/cpu-throttling: "false"' in text
    assert 'autoscaling.knative.dev/minScale: "1"' in text
    # Issue #471: per-instance SQLite state — a second instance splits the job queue.
    assert 'autoscaling.knative.dev/maxScale: "1"' in text
    # Reserved runtime env name — a manifest carrying it is rejected at deploy time.
    assert "CLOUD_RUN_JOB" not in text
    assert "RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS" in text
    assert "containerPort: 8080" in text
    # The wrapper must create the config dir BEFORE redirecting into it, and exec the server.
    wrapper = re.search(r'args: \["-c", "(.+)"\]', text).group(1)
    assert wrapper.startswith("mkdir -p /tmp/apphome && ")
    assert "export RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/apphome" in wrapper
    assert wrapper.endswith("exec python3 -m backend.main --server --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8080")

67	The API server (`/api/process`, `/api/compile` — #329) runs its **job worker IN-PROCESS**: the
68	request returns `202` and a background thread drains the job (GPU detection dispatches to the Job;
69	the reel stitch runs on the control plane itself). On Cloud Run that background thread only makes
70	progress while the instance has CPU, so a scale-to-zero **service** deploy MUST set:
71	```bash
72	gcloud run deploy rally-control-plane --image <image> --region $REGION \
73	  --no-cpu-throttling \        # CPU stays allocated between requests → the drain thread finishes
74	  --min-instances 1 \          # one always-warm instance drains reliably (cheap CPU; NOT the D7 GPU pool)
75	  --cpu 2 --memory 4Gi --port 8080 --allow-unauthenticated \
76	  --set-env-vars DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving,CLOUD_RUN_JOB=rally-worker,CLOUD_RUN_REGION=$REGION,GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=$PROJECT
77	```
78	Without `--no-cpu-throttling`, a `/api/compile` returns 202 and then **stalls** — the reel never
79	finishes and startup crash-recovery would mark it terminal. `min-instances 1` does NOT contradict

### 122. assistant (2026-07-04T09:23:12.212Z)

Yes — resolved, pushed, and CI-green on the PR; it's awaiting the owner's LGTM to merge.

To be precise about which race: `claim_due_job()` was already CAS-safe — two workers could never double-claim the same job. The actual bug (the reviewer's round-2 blocker) was a **false-empty**: when several drain workers SELECTed the same head-of-queue row, only one won the CAS update, and the losers returned `None`. `_drain_due_jobs()` treats `None` as "queue is empty" and exits — so with 8 queued jobs and 8 workers, only 2 got claimed and the rest sat stranded until some later submit or wake-up.

The fix (commit `21ee141` on [#468](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/468)): a lost CAS now **retries inside `claim_due_job()`** — re-SELECT the head runnable row, re-attempt the claim — until it either wins a row or a fresh SELECT genuinely finds nothing runnable. So `None` now means "truly empty" by construction, and `_drain_due_jobs()` needed no change. The retry loop is deliberately unbounded: every lost race means another worker successfully claimed that row, so the runnable set strictly shrinks and progress is guaranteed — a retry cap would just reintroduce the false-empty.

Proof it's actually fixed, not just plausibly:
- New regression test (`test_concurrent_claimers_claim_all_jobs_distinctly`): 8 queued jobs, 8 claimer threads released simultaneously on a `threading.Barrier`; asserts all 8 claims return non-None, the claimed ids are exactly the 8 distinct jobs, and a 9th claim returns `None`.
- With the fix stashed, that test reproduces the reviewer's exact failure 5/5 runs (`['j0','j1',None,…]` — same shape as their repro); with the fix it passed 15/15 consecutive runs, with no SQLite "database is locked" under the thread race.
- An independent adversarial verifier re-proved the test bites at the old head in a throwaway worktree before I pushed.
- Full suite 1634 passed / 10 skipped, ruff + mypy clean locally, and all 11 CI checks green on the new head.

The one related-but-separate item: the *park-vs-block* refinement (letting a compile job wait via `JobWaiting` instead of blocking a worker on the local-compute lane) was offered to the reviewer earlier and deliberately left out — they haven't asked for it.

1	# Cloud Build config for the Cloud Run GPU worker image (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M7).
2	#
3	# `gcloud builds submit --tag … -f …` does NOT work — `gcloud builds submit` has no `-f` flag, so a
4	# non-root Dockerfile needs a build CONFIG like this one. Build with:
5	#
6	#   gcloud builds submit --config=deploy/cloudrun/cloudbuild.yaml --project <PROJECT> .
7	#
8	# Override the region/repo/image with --substitutions=_REGION=…,_REPO=…,_IMAGE=… if needed.
9	# PROJECT_ID is provided by Cloud Build automatically.
10	substitutions:
11	  _REGION: asia-southeast1   # L4 GPU is region-limited (asia-southeast1 / europe-west1 / europe-west4 / us-central1 / us-east4)
12	  _REPO: rally
13	  _IMAGE: rally-worker
14	steps:
15	  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker'
16	    args:
17	      - build
18	      - -t
19	      - '${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/${_REPO}/${_IMAGE}:latest'
20	      - -f
21	      - deploy/cloudrun/Dockerfile
22	      - .
23	images:
24	  - '${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/${_REPO}/${_IMAGE}:latest'
25	options:
26	  machineType: E2_HIGHCPU_8   # the CUDA + micromamba image is heavy; a bigger builder keeps it ~8–10 min
27	timeout: 3600s
28

88	    """Start a chunked upload. Returns upload_id; PUT sequential parts next."""

90	    try:
91	        name = safe_output_filename(_normalize_upload_filename(req.filename))
92	    except ValueError as e:
93	        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(e)) from e

95	    if ext not in exts:
96	        raise HTTPException(
97	            status_code=400,
98	            detail=f"Extension '.{ext}' not allowed. Allowed: {sorted(exts)}",
99	        )
100	    if req.total_size_bytes is not None and req.total_size_bytes > max_bytes:
101	        raise HTTPException(
102	            status_code=413,
103	            detail=f"File exceeds the {max_bytes // (1024 * 1024)} MB upload limit.",
104	        )
105	    os.makedirs(upload_dir, exist_ok=True)
106	    # P2 (D11): fail fast with 507 if the box can't hold the declared upload. Best-effort — an
107	    # undeclared size (total_size_bytes=None) or an unreadable disk skips the guard (never blocks a
108	    # legit upload); the per-part total cap in upload_part is the hard backstop either way.
109	    require_free_bytes(upload_dir, req.total_size_bytes, stage="upload")
110	    upload_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
111	    tmp_path = os.path.join(upload_dir, f".partial-{upload_id}")
112	    open(tmp_path, "wb").close()
113	    with _uploads_lock:
114	        _uploads[upload_id] = {
115	            "filename": name,
116	            "tmp_path": tmp_path,
117	            "parts": 0,
118	            "bytes": 0,
119	        }
120	    return {
121	        "upload_id": upload_id,
122	        "max_part_mb": part_bytes // (1024 * 1024),
123	        "next_part": f"/api/upload/{upload_id}/part/0",
124	    }
125

127	@router.put("/api/upload/{upload_id}/part/{index}")
128	async def upload_part(upload_id: str, index: int, request: Request):
129	    """Append one part (raw bytes body). Parts are strictly sequential (0,1,2,...)."""
130	    _, max_bytes, part_bytes, _ = _upload_cfg()
131	    with _uploads_lock:
132	        st = _uploads.get(upload_id)
133	    if st is None:
134	        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Unknown upload_id '{upload_id}'")
135	    if index != st["parts"]:
136	        raise HTTPException(
137	            status_code=409,
138	            detail=f"Out-of-order part: expected {st['parts']}, got {index} (parts are sequential)",
139	        )
140	
141	    received = 0
142	    overflow = None
143	    with open(st["tmp_path"], "ab") as fh:
144	        async for chunk in request.stream():
145	            received += len(chunk)
146	            if received > part_bytes:
147	                overflow = (
148	                    f"Part exceeds the {part_bytes // (1024 * 1024)} MB per-part limit."
149	                )
150	                break
151	            if st["bytes"] + received > max_bytes:
152	                overflow = (
153	                    f"Upload exceeds the {max_bytes // (1024 * 1024)} MB total limit."
154	                )
155	                break
156	            fh.write(chunk)
157	    if overflow:
158	        _abort_upload(upload_id)
159	        raise HTTPException(
160	            status_code=413, detail=overflow + " Upload aborted — re-init to retry."
161	        )
162	
163	    with _uploads_lock:
164	        st["parts"] += 1
165	        st["bytes"] += received
166	    return {
167	        "upload_id": upload_id,
168	        "received_parts": st["parts"],
169	        "received_bytes": st["bytes"],
170	    }
171	
172	
173	@router.post("/api/upload/{upload_id}/complete")
174	def upload_complete(upload_id: str, req: UploadCompleteRequest):
175	    """Finalize: verify part count, move into place, return {path, video_id} for /api/process."""
176	    upload_dir, _, _, _ = _upload_cfg()
177	    with _uploads_lock:
178	        st = _uploads.get(upload_id)
179	    if st is None:
180	        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Unknown upload_id '{upload_id}'")
181	    if st["parts"] == 0 or st["parts"] != req.total_parts:
182	        raise HTTPException(
183	            status_code=409,
184	            detail=f"Expected {req.total_parts} part(s), received {st['parts']}.",
185	        )
186	    final_path = os.path.join(upload_dir, st["filename"])
187	    if os.path.exists(
188	        final_path
189	    ):  # never overwrite an earlier upload with the same name
190	        stem, ext = os.path.splitext(st["filename"])
191	        final_path = os.path.join(upload_dir, f"{stem}-{upload_id[:8]}{ext}")
192	    os.replace(st["tmp_path"], final_path)
193	    with _uploads_lock:
194	        _uploads.pop(upload_id, None)
195	    video_id = compute_video_id(final_path)

### 220. user (2026-07-04T10:49:01.164Z)

1	"""Google Cloud Storage backend — first-class object buckets.
2	
3	Uses ``google-cloud-storage``, imported lazily so ``import backend.storage`` never pulls it;
4	install with the ``storage-gcs`` extra. Credentials come from Application Default Credentials
5	(``GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`` pointing at a service-account JSON, or workload identity) —
6	never from config. A ``client`` may be injected for tests.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	from datetime import timedelta
12	from importlib import import_module
13	from typing import Any, Iterator, Optional
14	
15	from backend.storage.base import StorageBackend
16	from backend.storage.ref import StorageRef, StorageStat
17	
18	
19	class GCSStorage(StorageBackend):
20	    def __init__(
21	        self,
22	        *,
23	        client: Any = None,
24	        project: Optional[str] = None,
25	        signed_url_ttl: int = 3600,
26	        **_ignored,
27	    ) -> None:
28	        self._ttl = signed_url_ttl
29	        if client is not None:
30	            self._client = client
31	            return
32	        storage_module: Any = import_module("google.cloud.storage")
33	        self._client = storage_module.Client(project=project)
34	
35	    def _blob(self, ref: StorageRef):
36	        return self._client.bucket(ref.bucket).blob(ref.key)
37	
38	    def fetch_to_local(self, ref, dst=None, *, overwrite=False) -> str:
39	        if dst is None:
40	            raise ValueError("GCSStorage.fetch_to_local requires a dst path")
41	        import os
42	
43	        dst = str(dst)
44	        if os.path.exists(dst) and not overwrite:
45	            return dst

### 252. user (2026-07-04T11:13:52.941Z)

# A1 alpha serving deploy — hosted CUJ run report (cloud-serving)

**Date:** 2026-07-04 · **Region:** asia-southeast1 · **Project:** gen-lang-client-0306026826
**Scope:** A1 (ALPHA_LAUNCH_READINESS §7) — stand up the alpha serving endpoint and verify the
hosted `process → jobs → compile → download` CUJ against the WASB-only owned detector.

> Companion to `DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_2026-07-04.md` (PR #457, the GPU-batching runlog). That
> report proved the L4 worker + measured the batching win via the M6 dispatch **shim**; THIS report
> proves the deployed **control-plane service** end-to-end. They do not overlap.

## 1. What was deployed

| Resource | Value |
|---|---|
| L4 worker **Job** | `rally-worker` — nvidia-l4, 8 vCPU / 32Gi, `--no-gpu-zonal-redundancy`, `--max-retries 0 --task-timeout 3600`, GCS-FUSE mount `khelsutra-rally-serving`→`/gcs`, `WASB_WEIGHTS=/gcs/models/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar` |
| Control-plane **Service** | `rally-control-plane` — rev 00002, `--no-cpu-throttling`, `min/maxScale 1/3`, cpu 2 / mem 4Gi, port 8080, **private (IAM-gated, no allUsers)** |
| Service image | `asia-southeast1-docker.pkg.dev/…/rally/rally-control-plane:latest` (= `rally-worker:latest` + `google-cloud-run`; see §5 bug 1) |
| Service URL | `https://rally-control-plane-2bosgeukpa-as.a.run.app` |
| Config (via base64 startup wrapper → `RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/apphome/config.json`) | `deployment.profile=cloud-serving`, `compute_target=cloud_run`, `indexing.skip_ai_handoff=true`, `storage.backend=gcs`, `output_root=gs://khelsutra-rally-serving`, `input_backend=gcs`, `input_root=…/videos` |
| Env | `DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving`, `CLOUD_RUN_REGION=asia-southeast1`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=…`, `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=*` (CLOUD_RUN_JOB is reserved → code default `rally-worker` is correct) |

**Boot-crash trap avoided:** the `cloud-serving` preset forces `indexing.skip_ai_handoff=False`
(Gemini ON), which would trip the `AI_HANDOFF_KEY_MISSING` startup check (no Gemini key) and
crash-loop. Verified `loader.py:194` deep-merges `defaults < preset < config.json`, so the
explicit `skip_ai_handoff=true` wins. Also pinned `storage.backend=gcs` explicitly to satisfy the
`STORAGE_INCOHERENT` check. Boot log confirmed: `deployment profile ACTIVE: cloud-serving
(compute_target=cloud_run)` + `Uvicorn running on http://0.0.0.0:8080`.

## 2. Health / capabilities (authenticated via `gcloud auth print-identity-token`)

- `GET /api/health` → `200 {"status":"ok","sport":"badminton","default_segmenter":"yolo_hybrid"}`
- `GET /api/capabilities` → `segmenters` incl. `wasb_hybrid`; `deployment.cloud_available=true`;
  `gemini_key_present=false` (no crash — skip_ai_handoff honored); `gcs` backend usable.

## 3. gs:// bucket-path CUJ — PROVEN end-to-end

1. `POST /api/process {"video_path":"gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos/MahadevpuraSingles.MP4","segmenter":"wasb_hybrid","compute":"cloud"}` → `202 {job_id: d16654d1…}`
2. Poll `GET /api/jobs/{id}`: `validating → dispatching (cloud_run) → done`.
   - CP pulled + validated the video (format/resolution/pts/scene/blur/relevance all pass).
   - Dispatched L4 Job execution `rally-worker-k49kg`; worker log: `WASB in FULLY-LOCAL mode
     (skip_ai_handoff=True) … no gemini handoff, no API key needed`, `torch 1.11.0+cu113 cuda True`.
   - **Result: `success` — "Successfully indexed 48 play segments (cloud_run / 'wasb_hybrid')"**;
     outputs at `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/outputs/915473…/`.
   - Wall: created 02:12:42 → finished 02:26:27 (**~13m45s**, incl. L4 cold image pull + inference).
3. `POST /api/compile {video_id, segment_ids:[1..48], output_filename:"mahadevpura_wasb_highlights.mp4"}` → `202 {job_id: d947a9e6…}` → poll → **`success`**. In-process ffmpeg stitch (trim 48 clips w/ padding → concat → watermark) on the CP; **`Dynamic highlight reel saved … (166.0 MB)`**; wall ~3m26s (02:27:47→02:31:13, incl. re-fetch of the gs:// source).
4. `GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/mahadevpura_wasb_highlights.mp4` — the reel is served (correct `206`/`video/mp4`/etag/size headers), and the **full 174,038,655-byte reel was retrieved via <32 MiB Range requests and ffprobes as a VALID video: H.264 1920×1080 @ 29.97fps + AAC, duration 244.3s** (48 rallies). ⚠ **A single full `GET` returns edge-500** — Cloud Run's ~32 MiB response cap vs. the `FileResponse` fixed `Content-Length` (see §5 bug 3).

## 4. Notes / observations

- **In-process compile serializes behind process jobs** (drain `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1)`):
  a queued compile waits while a process job holds the worker in its bucket-poll. Fine for the alpha
  (low concurrency); worth noting for load.
- **WASB batch tuning is NOT forwarded on the dispatch path.** `_maybe_dispatch_remote` forwards only
  `skip_ai_handoff` (orchestration.py:274-283); the L4 job runs its baseline batch (not the preset's
  batch64/prefetch4), so the measured #457 −30.5% win is not applied via the deployed CP. Minor
  (correctness unaffected); follow-up = forward `indexing.wasb.*` overrides like `skip_ai_handoff`.
- `player_pool` / `max_frames` are dropped on the cloud path (already noted at orchestration.py:273).

## 5. Bugs surfaced + handled

# A1 alpha serving — post-merge e2e deploy test (cloud-serving)

**Date:** 2026-07-04 · **Region:** asia-southeast1 · **Project:** gen-lang-client-0306026826
**Scope:** A1 (ALPHA_LAUNCH_READINESS §7) closure — prove the five merged serving fixes
(#465 input jail, #467 upload staging, #468 drain concurrency, #469 signed-URL download,
#470 auth-in-base-image) plus the committed manifest generator (#474) on a REAL deployed build,
then restore the live edge-auth posture.

> Companion to `DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_a1-cuj_2026-07-04.md` (PR #464): that report stood the
> stack up and FILED #461/#463/#466; THIS report proves their fixes end-to-end on merged master and
> records the one new bug the run surfaced (#475, fixed by #476 mid-run).

## 1. What was deployed

| Resource | Value |
|---|---|
| Source | merged `master` — `195932f` (first build) → `3c97117` (final build incl. #476) |
| Worker image | `…/rally/rally-worker:latest` — build 1 `sha256:ed2fe448…` (195932f), build 2 **`sha256:31475f27…`** (3c97117); job re-pinned via `gcloud run jobs update` after each build |
| L4 worker **Job** | `rally-worker` — unchanged shape (nvidia-l4, 8vCPU/32Gi, FUSE `khelsutra-rally-serving`→/gcs) |
| Control-plane **Service** | `rally-control-plane` — rev **00005** (build 1) → rev **00006** (final digest `31475f27…`), deployed FROM THE BASE IMAGE directly (no derived PyJWT overlay — #470 proven), `maxScale=1` (#471 option 1) |
| Manifest | generated by the **committed** `deploy/cloudrun/gen_service_yaml.py` (#474) — `--edge-auth off` for the test window, `--edge-auth on` to restore |

## 2. Boot / health (rev 00005, edge-auth OFF, identity-token auth)

- `GET /api/health` → `200 {"status":"ok",…}`; `GET /api/capabilities` → `wasb_hybrid` present,
  `gemini_key_present=false` (skip_ai_handoff honored), ffmpeg/ffprobe present.
- Before-state also captured on the OLD rev 00004: `/api/process` without a CF JWT → **401**
  `edge auth: missing Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion header` (the M9 posture was live as documented).

## 3. CUJ on the FINAL image (rev 00006) — all green

1. **gs:// process** — `POST /api/process {gs://…/MahadevpuraSingles.MP4, wasb_hybrid, compute=cloud}`
   → 202 → `validating → dispatching (cloud_run) → done` — **48 play segments** (same count as the
   #464 run; job `18dc6502…`). <!-- TODO: wall time -->
2. **#461 upload staging** — chunked upload (`init → part/0 → complete`) of a 105,345-byte 720p clip
   → `POST /api/process` with the returned **local** path + `compute=cloud` → job staged it to
   `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos/d8335f51…/upload-e2e-720p.mp4` (byte-exact) and dispatched;
   **success** (0 segments — correct for a synthetic test pattern). NOT the pre-fix
   "cloud-serving needs a cloud input" hard-fail.
   - ⚠ Validation floor: a 640×360 test clip is rejected (`resolution_fps_check`, min 1280×720) —
     first attempt failed for that reason; harmless, but alpha docs should mention the floor.
3. **#468 drain concurrency, live** — the upload job staged + dispatched **while** the compile of
   the big reel was stitching on the CP (rev 00005 run) — remote dispatch overlapped the local
   compile lane exactly as designed; no serialization, no false-empty stall.
4. **compile** — `POST /api/compile {video_id, segment_ids:[1..48], "e2e.mp4"}` → 202 → done
   (~4m54s on rev 00005); reel **mirrored to
   `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/highlights/915473…/e2e.mp4` (174,046,228 bytes ≈ 166 MiB)**.
   <!-- TODO: rev 00006 compile time -->
5. **#463 signed-URL download** — `GET /api/highlights/915473…/e2e.mp4` →
   <!-- TODO: 307 + location + full download size + ffprobe -->

## 4. Bug surfaced mid-run: #475 → fixed by #476 (merged, redeployed)

On the FIRST attempt (rev 00005, build `ed2fe448…` = pre-#476), the #463 check **failed**:
`GET /api/highlights/...` → **500**, log:
`[highlights] signed-url path failed …: you need a private key to sign credentials`.

- The reel WAS mirrored correctly; the **keyless signBlob path** failed and the handler fell back
  to serving the 166 MiB file locally → Cloud Run's ~32 MiB cap → edge-500 (the original #463
  symptom, resurrected by a different root cause).
- **Root cause (verified live):** `GCSStorage.signed_url()` reused the storage client's own token
  for `iamcredentials.signBlob`; `google.cloud.storage.Client.SCOPE` is **devstorage-only**, which
  signBlob rejects (`ACCESS_TOKEN_SCOPE_INSUFFICIENT`) — and the branch's bare `except: pass`
  swallowed it, so the fallthrough raised the misleading private-key error. Environment was clean:
  the SA holds self-`serviceAccountTokenCreator` and `iamcredentials.googleapis.com` is enabled.
- **Why tests never saw it:** the fakes carry no `_credentials`; this was the FIRST run of #469's
  path on real compute credentials. Filed **#475**, fixed by **#476** (`_signblob_identity()` mints
  a fresh `cloud-platform`-scoped ADC credential; failures now LOGGED), verified 1659✓/85.46%,
  merged on green CI, rebuilt (`31475f27…`), redeployed as rev 00006.

## 5. Notes / observations (rough edges captured)

- **`services replace` with an unchanged manifest is a NO-OP** — it does not re-resolve `:latest`.
  After a rebuild, force a new revision (`gcloud run services update --image …:latest`) or stamp a
  revision annotation. Follow-up candidate: a `--revision-suffix` in `gen_service_yaml.py`.
- **A redeploy wipes the per-instance SQLite state** — `/api/highlights` 404s on the DB record
  check (`main.py:1052`) even though the reel mirror exists in gs://. Known + filed as **#471**;
  the CUJ was re-run on rev 00006 to regenerate state. Worth noting for alpha operators: a deploy
  invalidates in-flight jobs and past download URLs (the SPA library repopulates on re-process).
- Validation floor for uploads (≥1280×720) — see §3.2.
- The 422-before-401 quirk: a **malformed-JSON** body gets FastAPI's 422 before the edge-auth
  dependency runs; any valid request without a CF JWT still 401s. No exposure; noted for triage
  literacy.

   clip → `POST /api/process` with the returned **local** path + `compute=cloud` → the job staged
   it to `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos/d8335f51…/upload-e2e-720p.mp4` (byte-exact size
   verified in the bucket) and dispatched to the L4 → **success** (0 segments — correct for a
   synthetic test pattern). NOT the pre-fix "cloud-serving needs a cloud input" hard-fail.
   Verified on BOTH builds (`ed2fe448…` and the final `31475f27…`).
   - ⚠ Upload validation floor: a 640×360 test clip is rejected (`resolution_fps_check`,
     min 1280×720) — the first attempt failed on this; alpha docs should mention the floor.
3. **#468 drain concurrency, observed live** — the upload job staged + dispatched **while** the
   48-segment reel compile was stitching on the CP: remote dispatch overlapped the local compile
   lane exactly as designed (no serialization, no false-empty stall). On rev 00006 the two process
   jobs (gs:// + upload) also ran the drain concurrently.
4. **compile** — `POST /api/compile {video_id, segment_ids:[1..48], "e2e.mp4"}` → 202 → done;
   measured stitch wall on rev 00005: created 10:39:32 → finished 10:44:26 (**~4m54s**, incl. the
   gs:// source re-fetch); rev 00006 re-run equally green. Reel **mirrored to
   `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/highlights/915473…/e2e.mp4` — 174,046,228 bytes (~166 MiB)**.
5. **#463 signed-URL download — VERIFIED**:
   - `GET /api/highlights/915473…/e2e.mp4` → **`307`** →
     `https://storage.googleapis.com/khelsutra-rally-serving/highlights/…/e2e.mp4?X-Goog-Algorithm=GOOG4-RSA-SHA256&X-Goog-Credential=492532266377-compute@…&X-Goog-Expires=3600&X-Goog-Signature=…`
     (v4, signed AS the runtime SA via keyless signBlob — no key file anywhere).
   - `curl -L` through the redirect: **full 174,046,228 bytes in 8.1s** (single GET — no 32 MiB
     edge-500, no Range workaround), and the file **ffprobes valid**: H.264 1920×1080 @ 29.97 fps
     + AAC, duration 244.29s (the 48-rally reel).

## 4. Bug surfaced mid-run: #475 → fixed by #476 (merged, redeployed)

On the FIRST attempt (rev 00005, build `ed2fe448…` = pre-#476), the #463 check **failed**:
`GET /api/highlights/...` → **500**; log:
`[highlights] signed-url path failed …: you need a private key to sign credentials`.

- The reel WAS mirrored correctly; the **keyless signBlob path** failed and the handler fell back
  to serving the 166 MiB file locally → Cloud Run's ~32 MiB cap → edge-500 (the original #463
  symptom, resurrected by a different root cause).
- **Root cause (verified live):** `GCSStorage.signed_url()` reused the storage client's own token
  for `iamcredentials.signBlob`; `google.cloud.storage.Client.SCOPE` is **devstorage-only**, which
  signBlob rejects (`ACCESS_TOKEN_SCOPE_INSUFFICIENT`) — and the branch's bare `except: pass`
  swallowed the real error, so the fallthrough raised the misleading private-key message.
  Environment was clean: the SA holds self-`serviceAccountTokenCreator` and
  `iamcredentials.googleapis.com` is enabled — code bug, not config.
- **Why tests never saw it:** the fakes carry no `_credentials`; this was the FIRST run of #469's
  path on real compute credentials. Filed **#475** → fixed by **#476** (`_signblob_identity()`
  mints a fresh `cloud-platform`-scoped ADC credential; failures now LOGGED before falling back;
  regression tests reproduce the credential shape). Suite 1659✓ / cov 85.46% / ruff+mypy clean;
  merged on green CI per the standing agreement; rebuilt (`31475f27…`) and redeployed (rev 00006);
  §3.5 is the pass on the fixed build.

## 5. Notes / observations (rough edges captured)

- **`services replace` with an unchanged manifest is a NO-OP** — it does not re-resolve `:latest`
  after a rebuild. Force a new revision (`gcloud run services update --image …:latest`) or stamp a
  revision suffix. Follow-up candidate: `--revision-suffix` in `gen_service_yaml.py`.
- **A redeploy wipes the per-instance SQLite state** — `/api/highlights` then 404s on the DB
  record check (`backend/main.py:1052`) even though the reel mirror exists in gs://. Known, filed
  as **#471**; the CUJ was re-run on rev 00006 to regenerate state. Alpha-operator implication: a
  deploy invalidates in-flight jobs and past download URLs (re-process regenerates them).
- Upload validation floor (≥1280×720) — §3.2.
- 422-before-401 quirk: a **malformed-JSON** body gets FastAPI's 422 before the edge-auth
  dependency runs; any well-formed request without a CF JWT still 401s. No exposure; noted so a
  422 in edge-auth triage isn't misread.

## 6. Edge-auth restore + live posture (rev 00007, digest `31475f27…`)

- Redeployed with the generator's `--edge-auth on` manifest → rev **00007**.
- `GET /api/health` → **200** (deliberately exempt).
- `POST /api/process` (valid JSON, no CF JWT) → **401**
  `edge auth: missing Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion header`.
- `https://api.khelsutra.guru/api/health` (no CF session) → **302** →
  `https://khelsutra.cloudflareaccess.com/cdn-cgi/access/login/api.khelsutra.guru?…` with the
  Access-app aud `c980da67…082b` in the login meta — the exact pre-test posture, restored.

## 7. A1 DoD checklist

| DoD item | Status |
|---|---|
| Serving endpoint deployed + healthy | ✅ rev 00007, final digest `31475f27…` |
| Upload → process → jobs → compile → download CUJ | ✅ end-to-end on the final image |
| Edge-auth (M9) restored + verified | ✅ 401 sans JWT; api.khelsutra.guru → CF login 302 |
| >32 MiB download via signed URL (#463) | ✅ 307 + full 174 MB single-GET + ffprobe-valid |
| Browser-upload staging to gs:// (#461) | ✅ staged byte-exact + dispatched + success |
| Parallel drain (#466/#468) observed live | ✅ dispatch overlapped compile; 2 process jobs drained concurrently |

**A1 → DONE** (flipped in `docs/ALPHA_LAUNCH_READINESS.md` §7 in this PR).

**A1 is done — all PRs merged after full verification, and the end-to-end service run passed on the final deployed build with edge-auth restored.**

## What happened

**Merges (each verified before merging).** For every PR I ran the *merge result* against master locally in a temp worktree — full suite with coverage, ruff, mypy — before squash-merging, per your pre-LGTM gate. Baseline master was 85.40% coverage; the results: [#467](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/467) 1637 passed / 85.38% (−0.02pp, the new defensive `except` branch — noise), [#468](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/468) 1645 / **85.44%**, [#469](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/469) 1652 / 85.39%, [#470](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/470) 1652 + md-links clean, [#474](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/474) 1655 / 85.39%. All 11 CI checks were green on every head; the feared `orchestration.py`/`main.py` overlaps auto-merged cleanly and the suites proved no semantic conflicts. Issues #461/#463/#466/#473 auto-closed.

**The e2e (north star).** Rebuilt the worker image from merged master, re-pinned the L4 job, deployed the control plane from the base image via the newly committed `gen_service_yaml.py` — zero scratchpad artifacts, proving #470 and #474. Then ran the full CUJ:

- **gs:// video → 48 rallies** on the L4 (same count as the prior run), compile → reel mirrored to gs:// (166 MiB).
- **#461 verified:** chunked upload staged byte-exact to `videos/<id>/…` and dispatched — on both builds.
- **#468 verified live:** the upload job dispatched *while* the compile was stitching; two process jobs later drained concurrently.
- **#463 found broken, then fixed and verified.** The first redirect attempt **500'd on real credentials**: the keyless signBlob path rode the storage client's devstorage-scoped token (rejected by `iamcredentials`), a bare `except` swallowed the real error, and the handler fell back to local serving → the 32 MiB cap. Filed [#475](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/475), fixed in [#476](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/476) (fresh `cloud-platform`-scoped credential + loud logging; 1659 passed / 85.46%; merged on green CI per the standing agreement), rebuilt, redeployed. Result: **307 → signed `storage.googleapis.com` URL, full 174,046,228 bytes in 8.1s single GET, ffprobe-valid** 1080p29.97+AAC, 244.3s.
- **Edge-auth restored** (rev 00007, same fixed digest): health 200, process-without-JWT 401, `api.khelsutra.guru` → CF Access login 302 with the correct app aud. The stack is left running per your standing instruction.

**Record:** runlog + A1→DONE flip merged as [#477](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/477); master is `122f174`.

## Rough edges captured along the way

Fixed: #475 (signBlob scope). Filed and open: [#471](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/471) — per-instance SQLite state (it bit during the e2e: a redeploy wipes the DB so `/api/highlights` 404s despite the gs:// mirror; alpha runs maxScale=1) and [#472](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/472) — cert-renewal watch before ~Sept 30. Documented in the runlog: `services replace` with an unchanged manifest silently doesn't re-resolve `:latest` (a `--revision-suffix` for the generator is a good follow-up), and uploads below 1280×720 fail validation — worth a line in the alpha docs.

All worktrees and branches from this session are cleaned up, and the memory handoff points at the next tracks: the A13 launch review, the khelsutra UX backlog (async `/api/compile` #329), and the #471 durable-state fix.

GPU billing stops with the execution; the job row on the CP resolves ~30 min later or dies with the instance on the next redeploy (#471).\n\n**R5. Delete a user's data for a given `video_id`.** Bucket is the durable store; the DB is ephemeral.\n\n- *Bucket objects* (all three prefixes, plus the user's own source object if they supplied a raw gs:// path outside `videos/<video_id>/`):\n  ```\n  gcloud storage rm -r gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos/<video_id>/     # staged upload — orchestration.py:284 (input_root gen_service_yaml.py:61)\n  gcloud storage rm -r gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/outputs/<video_id>/    # worker outputs — a1-cuj §3.2 (\"outputs at gs://…/outputs/915473…/\")\n  gcloud storage rm -r gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/highlights/<video_id>/ # mirrored reels — orchestration.py:748-755\n  ```\n  Also sweep the dispatch done-markers (`<out_uri>/_dispatch/<token>.done`, `deploy/cloudrun/README.md:109-110`) — exact prefix in the serving bucket [unverified].\n- *DB reality:* rows live in per-instance ephemeral SQLite (#471) with **no delete API** (#447 open — the only known method is manual row surgery, impractical inside a Cloud Run instance).
